# Done queue items — archived from docs/OPEN.md (2026-09)

Historical record, not a work list. Every item here was ticked [x] in
docs/OPEN.md and was moved verbatim by `node scripts/archive-done.mjs --write`
(run by `npm run inventories:refresh`; docs/OPEN.md Q16). The queue tools still
read this file with docs/OPEN.md: the score line and next free number
(scripts/queue-count.mjs), the Everything-open block (scripts/scoreboard.mjs)
and src/test/queueItemsNameTheirGuard.test.ts. To REOPEN an item, move its
block back into docs/OPEN.md and untick it (a number in both files fails the
duplicate-number check).

- [x] **Q11 FIXED 2026-09-23 (branch cloud/q275-sentry), re-measure pending: the first hourly prod-errors sync after landing must log `sentry: N issue(s) with an event in the last 25h synced` and close the "sentry alerts are not synced" ledger item. ROOT CAUSE (measured, Sentry activity feed): Sentry itself resolves every helpr-4m issue 1 hour after its last event (`set_resolved_by_age`, age 1, by system) and reopens it on the next (`set_regression`); JAVASCRIPT-1H cycled 20 times 09-11..09-23; 2E/2F/2G were resolved the same way, nobody resolved them. AND the ledger sync never read Sentry at all: it used SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN (upload token) and got 403 every hour (prod-errors run 35895960717: "Sentry could not be read: Sentry 403"; ledger item "sentry alerts are not synced" open 9x), and asked only `is:unresolved`, which would have missed every auto-resolved issue. CHANGE: scripts/lib/sentryLedgerSync.mjs; the sync reads with SENTRY_READ_TOKEN (prod-errors.yml passes it), takes every issue with an event in the last 25h WHATEVER its Sentry status at its real lastSeen (Sentry status/substatus in sample_ref), and ops_alert_apply reopens a closed item on any newer occurrence (pinned by src/test/pglite/opsAlertLedger.pglite.mjs). GUARD: src/test/sentryLedgerSyncTracksRegressions.test.ts (5 @mutate, all red). OWNER OPTION (not needed for the ledger): Sentry project settings -> Auto Resolve (currently 1 hour) could be turned off so the Sentry UI stops reading "resolved". Was:** Sentry issues are "resolved" while they keep firing. JAVASCRIPT-1H
  "socket closed: 1005": 66 events, 4 users, last seen 1h ago, status resolved.
  The 09-22 profile-timeout issues -> "We couldn't load your account" error
  screen are also resolved with no fix recorded. Find out what resolves them

- [x] **Q13 DONE (2026-09-23): script-src has no `'unsafe-inline'`** in vercel.json,
  index.html's Capacitor meta or public/_headers. Measured inventory (built dist/):
  3 inline scripts in index.html (storage probe, boot watchdog, pre-paint theme),
  1 in offline.html, 1 in tools/apple-jwt.html, 2 JSON-LD data blocks (not
  executed, not governed), and ONE inline handler: the async-CSS
  `onload="this.media='all'"` from vite.config.ts. That handler was the trap:
  dropping 'unsafe-inline' alone left the whole app UNSTYLED (media=print, Times)
  in Chromium and WebKit. It is now a hashed swap `<script>`. Each inline script
  is allowed by its sha256. No runtime inline scripts (MapKit is loaded by
  src=; Stripe is a redirect; PostHog external loading is off). Verified on a
  prod build served with the vercel.json headers, both engines: boot, /login,
  /browse, /home (authed), /profile, MapKit map, Upgrade to Stripe sandbox
  checkout, boot watchdog reload, offline.html retry; a `javascript:` href, a
  script-inserted inline script and an `onerror=` handler are all blocked.
  Guards: `src/test/cspScriptSrc.test.ts` (source) and
  `scripts/check-csp-inline-scripts.mjs` (end of `npm run build` and `build:ios`,
  so Vercel refuses a bundle whose inline script lost its hash).
  Left open: (a) style-src still has `'unsafe-inline'`. React `style=` props and
  the boot shell need it; lower risk (no script execution). (b) Every page logs
  one `script-src eval` violation, and it was already there before this change:
  zod v4's `allowsEval` probe (`Function("")` in try/catch, forms chunk). It is
  harmless, and `z.config({ jitless: true })` would silence it. (c) public/_headers
  is Netlify-format and Vercel ignores it. It was kept in sync, but it is dead config.
  (d) Not device-tested in a native WKWebView. The Capacitor build's meta CSP was
  checked in desktop WebKit over http only.

- [x] **Q131 DONE (2026-09-23): offline mid-apply lost its confirmation, not its application — a stale-chunk recovery reload wiped "Application sent!".** Measured on prod as helper-e2e (evidence ~/.lh-shots/q131/, logs before*/after*): back online, Apply Now sent exactly ONE `apply_to_job` (200, row id returned) and exactly one applications row landed; then onSuccess's `track()` lazily imported the posthog chunk, whose fetch had failed while offline (the browser keeps a failed module fetch for the document's life), so `vite:preloadError` fired while online and main.tsx's recovery reloaded to `/home?_v=…` ~100ms after the 200 — toast gone, and the reloaded feed still listed the job the helper had just applied to. - [x] **Q154 DONE (2026-09-23): dashboard feed cards overlapped after a job was optimistically removed — VirtualizedJobList cleared its own row-size cache.** Measured on prod as helper-e2e with the funded fixture 1bb36551 (local build, evidence ~/.lh-shots/q154/): before the fix, applying from the job sheet (useApplyFlow onMutate drops the card) moved the row pitch from 110px to 92px at 375 and 114px to 92px at 1440, a 10px card overlap where there had been an 8px/12px gap, unchanged at +40s. Cause: `useEffect(() => virtualizer.measure(), [items.length])` in src/components/dashboard/VirtualizedJobList.tsx. In @tanstack/virtual-core 3.17 `measure()` CLEARS the per-key size cache, and rows that stay mounted are never re-measured (same node, same size: neither the ref callback nor the ResizeObserver fires), so every surviving row fell back to `estimateSize={92}`. Fix: the call is removed; sizes are cached by stable job id, so a new count re-lays the rows out on its own. No style changes. After: pitch 110 (375) / 114 (1440) held at +1.5s, +4s and +40s, gaps intact; before/after screenshots at both widths reviewed and recorded. Both test applications withdrawn (fixture back to 0 applications, open/escrow). **Guard:** src/components/dashboard/VirtualizedJobList.test.tsx renders the real virtualizer, removes an item and asserts every surviving row sits at least one measured row-height below the previous one (1 @mutate re-adding the `measure()` call; red: "expected 92 to be greater than or equal to 110").

- [x] **Q135 A plain whole-repo `npx vitest run` deadlocks on its own gate lock (found by the Q129 lane 2026-09-23).** src/test/gateLockGlobalSetup.ts is the root `globalSetup`, and both projects in vitest.config.ts (`unit`, `tz-sweep`) `extends: true`, so it runs twice in ONE process: the first call takes ~/.lh-gate.lock with this pid, the second finds the owner pid alive (itself) and waits (log printed "acquiring the gate lock" twice, then "waiting for pid 70270" = its own pid; hung 30+ min, killed). `npm run test:unit` / `node scripts/gateLock.mjs -- npx vitest run` are unaffected (LH_GATE_LOCK_HELD=1). Fix: make acquireGateLock re-entrant for `owner.pid === process.pid` (and release only on the last teardown), with a case in src/test/gateLockGlobalSetup.test.ts that calls setup() twice in-process. DONE 2026-09-23 (cloud/q135-q136): scripts/gateLock.mjs acquireGateLock is re-entrant: when owner.pid === process.pid it counts another hold instead of waiting, with the count kept per process on globalThis (each vitest project may load its own copy of the module); releaseGateLock removes the lock only on the LAST release. The lock path is now resolved per call, and LH_GATE_LOCK_PATH overrides it so the test never touches ~/.lh-gate.lock (a worker thread's process.env.HOME does not reach os.homedir()). Guard: src/test/gateLockGlobalSetup.test.ts "setup() twice in-process acquires twice and releases only on the last teardown" + 2 @mutate lines. RED on the old acquire/release logic (origin/main gateLock.mjs, only its path made lazy): "[gate-lock] waiting for pid 1209" = its own pid, then "Gate lock ... held by pid 1209 ... past the wait limit"; RED with early release (lock gone after the first teardown); GREEN 3/3.

- [x] **Q202 DONE 2026-09-23: card-dispute (chargeback) protection, all four owner decisions built.** (1) CLAW BACK: a real chargeback (charge.dispute.created, and the new charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn for an inquiry the bank escalates) on a job whose Helpr was paid reverses the Helpr's transfer(s) in transfer_group job_<id> up to the disputed amount (stripe-webhook/handlers/_chargebackClawback.ts), one chargeback_clawbacks row per (dispute, transfer) claimed before the Stripe call (migration 20260923154149, RLS: admins + the payee read, service role writes), Stripe idempotency keys, and on any resume Stripe is asked first (listReversals / the transfer group) so nothing is reversed or repaid twice past the ~24h key window; the job goes released -> chargeback by CAS before any reversal; a Stripe refusal (e.g. short balance) pages critical via the ops alert ledger, a transient failure 500s for redelivery; WON pays each reversed amount back (idempotent) and records the re-payment in payout_transfers as paid, LOST leaves it reversed; the payee is told in-app each time (role-neutral); transfer.reversed recognises its own clawback and does not freeze. (2) 3D SECURE 'any' on every Checkout Session from $300 (_shared/threeDSecure.ts: create-payment escrow, gift-card shortfall, tip; create-gift-card-checkout); the challenge runs on Stripe's hosted page on web and in the in-app browser sheet on native, and an abandoned challenge (open session, PI requires_action) can be paid again: create-payment expires the session and cancels the PI, refusing if it can no longer be canceled. (3) MAX JOB PRICE $1,000: ONE constant, _shared/jobBudgetLimits.ts, re-exported by src/lib/moneyLimits.ts; create-payment refuses a checkout outside $10-$1,000 before any session exists; migration 20260923154148 sets validate_job_budget() and the jobs_budget_range / jobs_urgent_fee_ceiling CHECKs to 1000 (measured first: 47 rows above $1,000, all is_seed, open/abandoned, no PaymentIntent; they are clamped to 1000, a real row would get NOT VALID instead; PGlite 3x); the post-job budget field says "The most a job can be is $1,000. Split a bigger project into separate jobs." and the step no longer shows Done above the cap (screenshots ~/.lh-shots/q202/, reviews recorded). (4) STANDARD PAY 3 DAYS after the job is marked done: _shared/escrowTiming.ts standardPayoutAtIso (done + 72h, never before now) used by auto-release-payment, create-payment's two-sided release and the re-pay checkout (anchored on the new charge); PAYOUT_HOLD_HOURS 48 after an auto-complete; every sentence that stated the timing now says "3 days after the job is marked done" (notifications, SubmittedStep, Earnings, Help Center, Terms); instant payout unchanged (it moves money already sent to a Helpr's Stripe balance, it does not shorten the 3 days). The admin stalled-job escalation stays at 48h on purpose. GUARDS (each proven red by mutation, 16/16, 5/5, 8/8, 6/6): src/test/edge/chargebackClawback.test.ts, src/test/threeDSecureOnLargeCharges.test.ts, src/test/jobBudgetCapIsOneConstant.test.ts (one constant: client + create-payment + newest DB trigger and CHECKs + seeds), src/test/standardPayoutThreeDays.test.ts. Reviews: lh-money-escrow and lh-silent-failure (REVIEW ONLY), every high/medium finding fixed and re-tested. Follow-ups: Q209 (owner step), Q210 (Q207 left for the Q189 findings its line names). Owner decisions: (1) "CLAW BACK: on charge.dispute.created for an already-released job, reverse the Helpr's transfer(s) for that job (idempotency key per dispute+transfer, ledger row, never double-reverse, handle partial/insufficient-balance errors loudly via the alert ledger). On charge.dispute.closed WON, re-pay the Helpr (idempotent). LOST: stays reversed. Tell the Helpr in-app (role-neutral copy)." (2) "3D SECURE on charges >= $300: request_three_d_secure 'any' (or 'challenge') on those PaymentIntents/Checkout sessions; make sure the client flow handles requires_action on web AND native." (3) "MAX JOB PRICE $1,000: client constant, server validators, the DB CHECK (replay-safe migration; existing rows above $1,000: measure first, only test data expected; do not break them), copy that states the cap." - [x] **Q178 DONE 2026-09-23: the current page's own chunk is requested in the FIRST round after the entry, and the likely next pages are warmed once it has loaded.** Measured (scripts/perf/measure-load.mjs: production build, 375, 1.6 Mbps / 150 ms RTT, 4x CPU, cold cache, gzip, median of 3; evidence ~/.lh-shots/q178/gz-base.json vs gz-E.json): / H1 4543 -> 2662 ms; /browse first card 5512 -> 4211 ms; /login form 4537 -> 2696 ms; /signup form 4752 -> 2978 ms. Causes found: vite.config.ts `resolveDependencies: () => []` emptied EVERY preload list (not just index.html's), so each lazy chunk walked its graph one round per level (20 JS rounds before the H1); App.tsx's lazy PageTransition suspended the page behind framer-motion (Index requested only after framer, at 3.9 s); ~45 KB gzip of route skeletons for OTHER routes on every cold load; useAuthReady fetched the 69 KB Sentry SDK mid-load for a breadcrumb it then dropped. Fix: tiny src/entry.ts starts main.tsx and the page chunk (src/boot/routePreload.ts) in the same tick, every dynamic import gets its preload list, preload-helper has its own chunk, PageTransition renders a plain keyed div until the animated one loads after the page settles, skeletons load on demand, cold-launch phases are buffered (src/lib/coldLaunchPhases.ts). Prefetch: src/lib/routePrefetch.ts LIKELY_NEXT_ROUTES (guest /browse /login /signup; signed-in /home /messages /jobs /post-job /profile) after load + 1 s network quiet, skipped on Save-Data / 2G. index.html's boot watchdog skips data-lh-page-preload links so a stale PAGE chunk keeps its in-app recovery (e2e/happy-path/stale-deploy.spec.ts + zz-runtime-probe chunk tests: 37 passed locally). Tried and reverted: withholding the page import's preload list (plain import()) to avoid the watchdog: +232 ms on / (2662 -> 2894). GUARD: scripts/perf/critical-path.mjs --check in .github/workflows/bundle-size.yml and npm run gate (budget scripts/perf/critical-path-budget.json, rounds exact both ways, KB band; red on origin/main: page chunk in round 7, ready after 11-12 rounds) + src/test/criticalPathBudget.test.ts (10 @mutate registrations, all killed). Was: Q178 LOAD SPEED: find what the ~4.5s on a slow phone connection is spent on (owner 2026-09-23: 'make sure everything is loading as fast as possible').** Measured (production build, 375, ~1.6Mbps/150ms): /, /browse and /login each take ~4.5s from navigation to real content, identical across routes. The entry bundle is small (index js 12KB gz, css 32KB gz), and starting the route chunk at module load did NOT move the number (4492->4502ms, reverted). So the time is a request WATERFALL (chained modulepreloads/vendor chunks, fonts, auth/session calls, first data fetch). WATERFALL MEASURED 2026-09-23 (landing, 375, throttled): 100 requests before the H1; the landing page's own chunk (Index) is requested only at ~3.8s, LAST. Ahead of it on the critical chain: ~20 route skeletons App.tsx imports statically (Dashboard/Activity/Earnings/Profile skeletons, NotificationPanel, tabs), app-shared 137KB, supabase 53KB (~2.2s), sentry 69KB (~3.5s), framer-motion proxy 38KB, MobileNav/DesktopSidebarNav/dialogs/sheet — in ~8 dependent rounds (0.6, 1.2, 1.4, 1.5, 1.7, 2.0, 2.1, 3.0s...). A preload at App module scope cannot help (App evaluates only after its static graph). Fix: make the shell's static graph minimal (route fallbacks and chrome lazy or per-route), keep sentry/framer/supabase off the landing critical path, and start the entry route's chunk before the shell graph (e.g. main.tsx kicks it off before importing App, or a modulepreload for it). Then re-measure; record a network waterfall per route, cut the chain (parallelise, inline critical, defer non-critical, preconnect/early-hint Supabase), and prove each route's number moves. Guard: a CI performance budget per entry route on the throttled profile.

GUARD: src/test/pglite/cronCatchUp.pglite.mjs (reschedule, pause, lock_timeout and query_canceled cases; red on 20260923145516: 15 FAIL; each change also mutated red) + src/test/cronCatchUpPolicy.test.ts (structural, @mutate on the new migration). Was:** Hardening findings of the Q189 lh-silent-failure review of run_missed_cron_catch_up() (migrations 20260923133021 + 20260923145516, policy seed 20260923145117). (1) DONE, MEDIUM: a RESCHEDULED daily/weekly job looked like a missed slot (slot from the current schedule; any earlier run counted as proof), so moving charge-recurring-visits (catch_up=false) to a later time would file a 'cron-missed-slot' error for a day it already ran, and a catch_up=true job would run twice that day. Now a slot needs proof the job was on THIS schedule: a failed run started at the slot, or an earlier run AND (a tick saw it on this schedule at or before the slot: new table cron_catchup_schedules; or a run / catch-up one period earlier). (2) OPEN: record the pg_net request id of a caught-up HTTP job so its HTTP outcome can be checked (today 'caught_up' means the request was queued, not that it succeeded). Waits for Q174, which is changing how cron HTTP requests are tagged. (3) DONE: the tick's 200ms lock_timeout applied to the EXECUTEd job; the job now runs with the session's lock_timeout and 200ms is restored after the block. (4) DONE: EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS does not catch query_canceled, so a statement_timeout rolled back the whole tick (every claim, retried every 10 min); now caught per command, recorded catch_up_failed and alerted, the tick continues.

  instead of the per-account cap (5) and spends the shared guest budget. Fix:

  role authenticated, so a signed-in client cannot erase its identity; guard
  red on the unstamped definition. DONE 2026-09-23 (20260923094457, from the
  live pg_get_functiondef): stamp_error_log_origin sets NEW.user_id :=
  auth.uid() for role authenticated (still SECURITY INVOKER). The only client
  writer, src/lib/errorLogger.ts, sends the id from the localStorage session
  blob or null when it cannot read one; either way the row is now the JWT's.
  Guard: src/test/errorLogClientIdentityAndThrottle.test.ts (@mutate);
  behaviour: src/test/pglite/userErrorScreenRepeatCap.pglite.mjs (auth insert
  with user_id NULL -> stored as the caller, ledger count 5, was 20).

Per path: auto-release-payment selects live statuses only and hands off to release-payout; release-payout, process-scheduled-payouts, void-cancelled-payments, create-payment cancel_escrow and admin_refund_general run checkUnsettledDispute (fails closed) before any money call; create-payment admin_release_dispute/admin_refund_dispute need status='disputed' plus claim_dispute_settlement, which refuses a decided-unexecuted dispute; auto-resolve-disputes needs status='disputed' plus a 'sweep' claim; instant-payout, cash-out-credits, charge-recurring-visits, onboarding fee and chargeback clawback do not touch a job's held escrow; money-reconciliation only reads. Live: 0 decided disputes with execution_status <> 'executed'. Moving the job transition into execute-dispute-split was rejected: it would change what the UI, money-reconciliation (Q336) and batch RPCs see and add no money safety. The status and copy problem ('resolved' shown before money moves) is filed as Q344. GUARD: src/test/decidedDisputeEscrowOnlyMovedBySplit.test.ts. Its exact inventory of every edge file with a Stripe money call is 10 files, each classified. Each unsettled-check path must act on .blocked (return/continue/throw) before every money call in its region, including local helpers and collect-then-pay loops. The create-payment actions that move money must equal the map exactly, each gated. The split must claim before money. node scripts/vacuity/index.mjs --only on it: 9/9 mutations killed (~/.lh-shots/q231/vacuity2.txt). An lh-money-escrow REVIEW ONLY found v1 did not check that .blocked was acted on; fixed. Its other findings are filed as Q342-Q343.

- [x] **Q267 A retried job post can create a SECOND job (found by Q68, code read 2026-09-23).** src/pages/post-job/useJobSubmit.ts:431 inserts `jobs` with no idempotency key and there is no unique index to stop a repeat (only jobs_one_visit_per_series_date, recurring visits only). If the insert's response is lost, the error path clears submittingRef and sets no cooldown, so pressing again inserts a second row. In the post+pay flow, a lost create-payment response makes handleSubmit delete the first job (cleanupOrphanJob) and a retry posts a NEW job with a new Checkout Session; the first session (key `escrow-<old jobId>`) is never expired. Fix needs a client-generated key sent with the insert and a unique index on (customer_id, key) (data-model + money: lh-money-escrow + lh-silent-failure review). Proof: slow-network post·drop and pay-start·drop (e2e/slow-network/slow-network.spec.ts) go green.

Layers read: client write (useJobSubmit), RPC/edge (create-payment re-mint gate + session expiry), DB (jobs indexes, INSERT/SELECT grants, jsonb_populate_record SETOF jobs RPCs unaffected), reader (JOB_READABLE_COLUMN_LIST). PGlite 3x: old state 2 jobs for one retried request; after, the retry is 23505 and authenticated can SELECT the key. FIXED 2026-09-26 at the shared layer.** Root cause: EditJobDialog echoes the typed title as its DialogHero. DialogContent is a `grid` whose implicit `auto` column floors at its widest child's min-content, so one unbroken 200-char word widened the column past the card. MEASURED with the built CSS and the exact DialogContent/DialogHero/Input classes at 375: dialog scrollWidth 2202 > clientWidth 341, fields' right edge at 2203 against a 359 card edge, and documentElement still 375, which is why the sweep missed it (measureLayout scans `#root *`; dialogs are portaled to body). FIX: DialogContent `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)]`; DialogHero and SheetHero titles `[overflow-wrap:anywhere]`. After: 341 = 341, fields inside, title wraps (q273-before/after reviewed). A normal dialog renders byte-identical before and after at 375 and 1440. GUARDS: src/components/ui/dialogShell.test.ts "no child can widen a popup" (two @mutate, each red); e2e/prod-audit/harness.ts health() now runs readDialogOverflow on every open dialog whenever layout is asserted (the item's asked-for dialog-internal assertion; 2202>341 before, none after). - [x] **Q293 A scheduled prod-load run can be cancelled with nothing reported (found by the Q70 review, 2026-09-23).** GitHub keeps one PENDING run per concurrency group; a third run entering prod-load cancels the waiting one at workflow level, so no job (not even notify) runs and no nightly-red issue is filed. For the monthly privacy journey that is a missed month. schedule-heartbeat.yml derives each workflow's cadence from its cron (weekly here, because of the first-7-days gate), so it cannot tell that the one DUE week was cancelled. Fix: have the privacy journey's green notify record the month it ran (e.g. the nightly-red issue sync, or a dated marker), and alarm when the current month has none after day 8. Guard: a test on that check.

Measured live (dev server, poster-e2e session, 375 width): computed `padding-bottom` 112px both before and after, `documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` (500 === 500, no overflow), screenshots pixel-identical. src/components/profile/LegalTab.tsx:136 (`ProfileTabBody bottomClearance="calc(env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px) + 6rem)"`) now reads `"calc(var(--safe-area-bottom, 0px) + 6rem)"` — NOT the full pb-safe-nav value: Profile.tsx's own tab scroll container already reserves the full dock clearance for every tab (`pb-[calc(var(--safe-area-bottom,0px)_+_96px_+_1rem)]` at Profile.tsx:785), so `bottomClearance` is EXTRA room on top of that baseline for the tab's own content (matches the sibling `ProfileEditForm.tsx:153`, `bottomClearance="calc(var(--safe-area-bottom, 0px) + 6.5rem)"`, already on this pattern); using the full token here would have reserved the dock height twice. - [x] **Q332 Offline cold /browse says "Showing the last data we have" over bare skeletons (slow-network browse·drop screenshot, run 35940421547, 2026-09-24).** With no cached data the banner promises data that is not there, and the list shows loading skeletons. Not yet measured: whether the skeletons stay up indefinitely offline. Fix: a designed offline-and-nothing-cached state (lh-audit §3 offline), copy that matches it. Guard to name when fixed: browse·drop asserts no skeleton after N s offline with an empty cache. **DONE 2026-09-26 (cloud/open-perf-client).** MEASURED (preview build, Linux Chromium, 375, real prod backend, jobs request held 4 s then the context set offline): BEFORE, skeletons held to +23.5 s, then "Nothing today, neighbor. New jobs post throughout the day" (a false empty state) under "You're offline. Showing the last data we have.", and never an offline or error state. So the skeleton did not stay up forever; it ended in a false claim. ROOT CAUSE: `useArrivalGate(!isLoading, …)`. In TanStack v5 isLoading = isPending && isFetching, and a query PAUSED offline is not fetching, so "not loading" with no data read as "loaded, zero jobs". FIX: src/lib/feedPhase.ts decides ready / error / offline-empty / loading from status + fetchStatus + online, and /browse renders a designed offline state (WifiOff, "You're offline.", "Open jobs will load here as soon as you're back online.", Try Again). The global banner now says "You're offline. Reconnect to load anything new.", which is true on every screen. AFTER: the offline state from +1.5 s, 0 skeletons, no empty state; back online, the cards load (12 headings); a warm page going offline keeps its cards (screenshots browse-offline-before/after reviewed). GUARDS: src/test/offlineWithNothingLoaded.test.ts (feedPhase cases, /browse uses it, banner copy; red 2 of 6 on the pre-fix page/banner, 4 of 4 @mutate killed); live: the new "cold, nothing cached" step in e2e/slow-network browse · drop (nightly on prod; not run here, no poster/helper creds in this container). Other screens with the same `isLoading`-means-loaded shape are unverified: Q571.

MEASURED (vacuity run 36210230529, job 108317883737, 2026-09-26, the first with Q412's failureTail): the ONLY offender is `profile-landing` (/profile) at every phone width 320/375/390/430 — `header→title 0px, the shells' --shell-gap is 12` and `title→content 0px, want 12 (--shell-gap); first content <div.relative> PPBaton Rouge, LA·New member…`; the :297 section-gap test also failed (its assertion not printed). - [x] **Q327 A ban never settles the banned user's live jobs (found by the Q301 lh-money-escrow review, 2026-09-23).** admin-user-actions/index.ts:563 cancels only pending applications, and no public SQL function that reads ban_status cancels or refunds a job (pg_proc source search, per the reviewer). A counterparty can be left on an accepted job with a banned user. Live exposure measured by the reviewer: 0 live jobs with a banned party. Decide what a ban does to live jobs (cancel with refund, or hand to admin) and add it to the ban path. Guard to name when fixed: a PGlite case where banning a user with an accepted job leaves that job settled. **FIXED 2026-09-26** (migration supabase/migrations/20260927012042_permanent_ban_settles_one_off_jobs.sql, ported from origin/wip/backup-q327 06d206b41 with a fresh timestamp). An AFTER UPDATE OF ban_status trigger on profiles (fires on 'banned'/'permanently_banned', every writer) runs settle_one_off_jobs_for_banned_account(user) (service_role only), which decides each job with ban_settlement_action(seat, status, payment_status, work started, crew, series), a pure function whose ELSE is 'unhandled' (pages admins). Outcomes: POSTER banned, funded, no work started -> cancelled with poster_cancel_job's own pricing (void-cancelled-payments then pays a committed Helpr the late fee, refunds the rest); POSTER banned, never paid -> cancelled $0; HELPR banned, no work started -> job reopened, escrow kept, their accepted application closed silently (party_blocked); EITHER banned after work started -> platform-filed ESCALATED dispute (nothing auto-pays until an admin decides); already disputed -> escalated; money already moving, a crew, or a finished/cancelled job still owing the banned Helpr -> nothing changed on the job, admins alerted per job (the crons still pay or refund on schedule: this is NOT a hold, see Q327-b); series left to the recurring lane. Pending applications from the banned account closed silently; a pending offer to it declined and its poster told. Each job settles in its own subtransaction, the applications/offers step and every admin alert in theirs, and the trigger itself swallows any error, so a ban never fails or rolls back because of settling. GUARDS: src/test/banSettlesOneOffJobs.test.ts (class guard: every job_status and every jobs_payment_status_check value bucketed in ban_settlement_action's newest definition, two-way; pricing identical to poster_cancel_job; escalated hold; trigger values; revokes; 7 @mutate, 7/7 killed); src/test/edge/void-cancelled-payments.banSettle.test.ts (a ban-cancelled job settles like a poster cancel; 2 @mutate, 2/2 killed); behavioural src/test/pglite/banSettlesOneOffJobs.pglite.mjs (`npx tsx`): R0-R4 RED before (a ban settles nothing), migration applied 3x, A1-A18 green, CLASS SWEEP 352 combinations 0 bad. REVIEW: lh-money-escrow (opus, review only) no blocker/high; its 2 mediums (post-loop steps and admin alerts could roll back the ban -> wrapped; admin_review comment/alert overstated a money hold -> corrected) and 1 low (reopen's application rejection now party_blocked) fixed before landing, proof re-run green. OWNER QUESTIONS (conservative path built): (Q327-a) banned party after work started: pay for work done? Built: escalated dispute, admin decides per job; recommend keep. (Q327-b) banned Helpr's finished payout or late-cancel fee still to be paid: pay it? Built: paid on schedule by the crons, admins alerted per job so a person can stop it; recommend pay earned money unless the ban is for fraud, and consider a real payout hold (none exists today). (Q327-c) TEMPORARY ban and one-off jobs: nothing happens; recommend alerting admins when a temp-banned Helpr has a booking inside the ban. (Q327-d) banned poster's accepted booking cancelled late pays the Helpr the poster_cancel_job late fee; recommend keep. LIVE VERIFY after db-deploy: `SELECT proname, proacl FROM pg_proc WHERE proname IN ('ban_settlement_action','settle_one_off_jobs_for_banned_account','settle_one_off_jobs_on_permanent_ban')` (service_role + postgres only) and `SELECT tgname FROM pg_trigger WHERE tgname='trg_settle_one_off_jobs_on_permanent_ban'`.

- [x] **Q307 FIXED on cloud/q307-q348 2026-09-26, lands with the PR (then [x]): every template now proves its EVENT from DB state, not only its sender.** VERIFIED 2026-09-26: fix commit e2fcbd695 (PR #1820) confirmed an ancestor of origin/main via merge-base; guard src/test/edge/create-notification.test.ts confirmed present on disk; the item's own tick condition ("lands with the PR") is met. supabase/functions/_shared/notification-templates.ts: work_started = job in_progress + the Helpr's job_tracking row "working"; dispute_withdrawn / dispute_resolved = newest disputes row withdrawn AND opened by the sender (rpc_withdraw_dispute lets only the opener close it); dispute_response = jobs.dispute_status live + dispute_helper_response (not the disputes row: 2 live jobs have an active dispute_status and no disputes row, measured 2026-09-26); revision_acknowledged = newest job_revisions row accepted (HelperRevisionCard no longer sends it on the legacy revision_note path, which records nothing: 2 such jobs live); job_confirmed = the SENDER's own stamp (poster_confirmed_at / helper_dayof_confirmed_at); arrival_confirmed / work_confirmed = poster_confirmed_arrival_at / poster_confirmed_working_at; job_offer = target application accepted. create-notification reads those columns and answers 500 (not 409) when a fact read errors. GUARD: src/test/edge/create-notification.test.ts "Q307" block — STATE table must name EXACTLY the NOTIFICATION_TEMPLATES keys; per template "state not reached → 409, nothing inserted" and "state reached → lands". Red on the pre-fix templates: 9/12 fail (exactly the 9 named here; the 3 already-proven stay green); vacuity 8/8 killed. **lh-authz-rls REVIEW-ONLY pass done 2026-09-26, fixes in the same PR:** F2 dispute_resolved (type payment, "Payment will be released") now also needs payment_status payout_pending/released (live job bb2c3732 had the dispute withdrawn by the poster with escrow still held); F3 revision_acknowledged now needs the revision requested_by = the poster (job_revisions RLS lets the assigned Helpr insert rows; all 26 live rows are by the poster); F5 one-conjunct cases (dispute withdrawn by the other party, released-escrow, job status for work_started, empty dispute response, poster side of job_confirmed) + an assertion that the disputes / job_tracking / job_revisions reads are scoped to the job (and the caller), since the edge mock does not match on filters. Vacuity 13/13 on the file; scoping assertion red by hand with the disputes job_id filter removed. Not fixed, by design (F4): proofs are durable state, so a true notice can be re-sent while the state holds, bounded by the rate limit — documented in the template header. Was: **Q307 Notification templates check WHO sends, not WHETHER the event happened (Q223 follow-on, 2026-09-23).** Only revision_requested, application_declined and no_show_reported read the database to prove their event (a job_revisions row, a rejected application, a no_show strike). The other nine are gated to the right side of the job but not to its state: the assigned Helpr can send "Dispute withdrawn" on a job with no dispute, and the poster can send "Dispute resolved ✓ … Payment will be released" (type payment) before resolving. The words are all server-built and true-shaped, so the harm is a misleading notice about the sender's own job, not a phishing primitive. Fix: a state predicate per template in supabase/functions/_shared/notification-templates.ts (dispute_status, arrival / working stamps, poster/helper confirmation stamps), or move each notice into the RPC that performs the transition. Guard: extend src/test/edge/create-notification.test.ts with one "state not reached → 409" case per template.

## Archived 2026-09-27 — from "AUDIT BUS — findings live in the ledger, not here"

- [x] **Q756 N-006 follow-up: the bell's count and its realtime live only inside a mounted `NotificationPanel`.** On a phone only `DashboardTitleBar` mounts the bell. On a cold launch to another page (for example a push tap into /messages), the store is unbound, and the client leaves the server's push-set badge in place. After leaving the dashboard the store's notification count freezes. Fix: move the head count and subscription into a provider mounted for every signed-in user, and drop the "no badge until the bell counts" gate in `useNavUnreadCount`. Done when a test proves the badge updates with no bell mounted. **DONE 2026-09-27 (81ca72513):** the feed (load + head count + the one `notifications:insert` binding) moved to `src/components/notificationPanel/notificationFeed.ts`, a per-user refcounted feed opened by `useNavUnreadCount`, which MobileNav runs on every route; the panel only renders the store and keeps chime/local push through `onNotificationArrival`, and hears a reconnect reload's failure or recovery through `onFeedRecoveryLoad` (lh-silent-failure note). The not-yet-counted badge gate was KEPT on the lead's instruction (it now clears ~800ms after launch on any route). Counted types unchanged (Q755 pending). Guard: `src/test/bellFeedBoundOnEveryRoute.test.tsx` (no panel -> badge moves; 2 navs + panel -> ONE binding; source floor), red on main, vacuity 1/1 killed.
  RE-MEASURED LIVE 2026-09-27 after db-deploy (6c98f9498, success): trigger present (1); unread type=message
  notifications 221, and all 221 belong to a thread that still has an unread message to that user, so 0 are stale.

## Archived 2026-09-27 — from "AUDIT BUS — findings live in the ledger, not here"

- [x] **Q755 N-006 follow-up (owner decision): message notifications are counted twice in the badge sum.** Every chat message also inserts a `type='message'` notification that is not marked read when the thread is read, so badge = messages + bell counts those twice. Measured on prod 2026-09-27 (read-only SQL): 327 unread notifications, 221 of them `type='message'`, and 8 unread non-system messages. The bell shows those rows too, so today the badge agrees with the app. Owner to choose: exclude `type='message'` from the bell and the badge, or mark those rows read when the thread is read. Done when chosen and the badge guards are updated. — DONE 2026-09-27 (owner chose "Mark read with thread"): migration `20260927064800_q755_mark_message_notifications_read_with_thread.sql` adds trigger `trg_mark_message_notifications_read` (a message flipping to read marks the receiver's `type='message'` notifications for that thread read; only the receiver or server code can trigger it, so a sender editing their own message cannot clear the receiver's bell) and backfills notifications whose thread has no unread message. PGlite: applied 3×, backfill/thread/sender-blocked cases pass. Guard: `src/test/messageNotificationsClearWithThread.test.ts` (every producer's link shape is matched by the marker), vacuity killed. Live re-measure of the 221 unread `type='message'` rows is due after db-deploy.

## Archived 2026-09-27 — from "MORNING QUESTIONS (held overnight 2026-09-23 while the owner sleeps)"

- [x] **Q77 FIXED 2026-09-23 (branch cloud/q77-q89-retry), live effect on the Mac not yet measured. CHECKED 2026-09-26: fix on main, guard present; the item's own tick condition is the owner's local Mac hygiene log, which I cannot read from this worktree — remains open. Finished agents' worktrees pile up.** Every worktree-isolated agent
  leaves a LOCKED worktree under .claude/worktrees/, and prune-git-hygiene
  never touches locked ones (by design, since running agents lock theirs). Add a
  safe rule: a worktree whose agent has finished (its branch is merged into
  origin/main or has no commits, no process has cwd there, and it's older
  than 2h) is unlocked and removed; anything unmerged is reported, never deleted.
  DONE: rule in scripts/lib/worktreeHygiene.mjs, called by
  scripts/prune-git-hygiene.mjs. A LOCKED worktree under <main>/.claude/worktrees/
  is unlocked and removed (`git worktree remove`, never --force) when HEAD is in
  origin/main (rev-list 0, or every commit upstream by patch-id via `git cherry`),
  status is clean, no process cwd is inside (lsof; unknown = remove nothing), the
  pid in its lock reason (if any) is not running, and it is older than 2h. Unmerged
  or dirty ones are REPORTED in the hygiene output/log, never removed. Main is never
  a candidate; locks elsewhere are still skipped. GUARD:
  src/test/pruneAgentWorktrees.test.ts (fixture repo: bare origin + clone + 7 linked
  worktrees; plan AND apply; 11 @mutate, all killed, incl. restoring the old
  "skip every locked worktree" rule). TO TICK: the next session-start hygiene log
  (~/.lh-hygiene/hygiene.log) on the owner's Mac shows the .claude/worktrees count
  falling; the Claude Code lock-reason format (pid or not) was not observable here. MEASURED 2026-09-27 (lane A), TICKED: ~/.lh-hygiene/hygiene.log, run 2026-09-26T04:16Z logged "remove 3 worktree(s) (3 locked agent worktree(s) unlocked first)", so the locked-agent rule fires live; the 09-27T16:10Z run removed 11 and skipped 34; `.claude/worktrees` holds 12 entries now. GUARD: src/test/pruneAgentWorktrees.test.ts.

- [x] **Q407** (OWNER DECISIONS, group + recurring jobs, owner 2026-09-25, answered in the session; these settle Q394, Q395 and the recurring lane's owner questions): GROUP: (1) a crew has NO lead: every hired member is equal in access (address, messaging the poster), pay share, cancellation-fee share and review; (2) a late poster cancellation's fee is split EVENLY across the hired crew; (3) one review PER Helpr, each counting toward that Helpr's own score and tier. RECURRING: (4) the poster chooses AT POSTING TIME between 'one person for every visit' and 'OK to split the days'; (5) when split, the first hired Helpr picks the visit dates they want, the poster then offers the remaining dates to the next Helpr they choose, and so on until every date is covered; a date nobody has picked stays offerable while the poster keeps offering it, and if it is still unfilled when it arrives it is not charged; (6) a Helpr giving up one of their dates (or ending their part of a series) gets a reliability strike ONLY within 24 hours of that visit, same as a late single-job cancel, and the date goes back to the poster to offer again; (7) a poster with a running series CANNOT delete their account until the series is ended; (8) after hire, a one-time job's date/time can change only as a request the Helpr accepts; (9) banning an account ends every series it posts or works on (future visits cancelled and not charged, the other party told). Build: group redesign on top of the reviewed roster-departure rework (group-fix 212b6db40); recurring split-days on top of recurring-fix. Each ships with a class guard and a money/authz review. **GROUP (1)-(3) BUILT 2026-09-25 on branch group-fix, NOT deployed, NOT pushed (migration 20260925154606_group_crew_has_no_lead + edge + client):** jobs.helper_id is NULL on every group job, enforced in every context by trg_group_job_has_no_lead (backfill keeps any former lead on the roster); helpers_needed / is_group_job locked once funded or hired (money review HIGH-1); each member's share frozen in cents at hire (group_job_helpers.slot_no / share_cents, largest remainder: $100/3 = 3334+3333+3333, MEDIUM-3) and paid from it by process-scheduled-payouts; every hire needs a funded job; proof photos: every member uploads to and reads the job folder, written to their own roster row through rpc_group_member_set_proof (PhotoProof crew path, HelperPhotoAsk reads the member's own slot); completion/cancel/payout notices go to every member; cancel_escrow re-reads the roster after its claim and pages on a stuck claim; an under-filled crew completes when every HIRED member is done and the unfilled shares are refunded to the poster (rule crew_completes_when_hired_done(), default true, owner to confirm, MEDIUM-4); tips: manual tip names a roster member (ReviewForm's 5-star prompt tips the reviewed member), auto-tip splits the poster's auto-tip across the crew (one claim per member); money-reconciliation grades a crew member by member (crew_transfers_exceed_escrow, crew_member_unpaid_on_released_job, per-member tier). D4: SeriesStrip used the UTC date as today, so from 18:00 CST / 19:00 CDT it skipped the next charge and named the one after. D5: a series whose poster deleted their account (customer_id NULL) stayed in cron scope and paged daily. Fix: require the sender to still be a party (hired, pending applicant, or crew member via group_job_helpers) or within the post-completion 24h window; PGlite case for a removed crew member and a rejected applicant. **FIXED 2026-09-25 (branch msg-authz, not yet live), extended the same day by the owner's both-ways rule (Q407 addendum 14):** (1) 20260925175953_message_gate_poster_first_requires_current_party.sql restates can_message_in_job from its newest text (20260914201350; 20260919220233 changed only job_messaging_closes_at; 20260925154606_group_crew_has_no_lead.sql mentions it only in a comment) with branch 4 also requiring a live application (status pending or accepted). (2) 20260925230845_messaging_closes_both_ways_off_job.sql adds is_off_job(job, user), an internal predicate: the user applied or was offered the job and is no longer a party (not the poster, not hired, no pending offer, not on the crew, no live application). It restates can_message_in_job from (1) with branch 2 admitting an offeree only while direct_offer_status = 'pending' (settles Q420(a)), restates can_send_message_to_in_job from 20260914215014 with AND NOT is_off_job(job, receiver) (settles Q420(b): the poster can no longer message someone who is off the job), and adds get_off_job_thread_state(job, other), which the client reads. It returns 'self' when the caller is off the job, 'other' when the other person is and the caller already has a thread with them, else NULL. Grants: can_message_in_job and is_off_job are service_role only; can_send_message_to_in_job and get_off_job_thread_state are authenticated + service_role; every REVOKE names PUBLIC and anon. CLIENT (review M2): src/lib/offJobGate.ts. ChatView asks get_off_job_thread_state and ChatComposer replaces the composer with a read-only notice ("This conversation is closed because you're / they're no longer on this job. You can still read everything here.", not role-based) on both sides, ahead of the poster-first lock. sendHandlers turns a 42501 RLS refusal the server attributes to off-the-job into a non-retryable `refused` bubble, not the retryable "didn't go through" one. The stale ChatView comment on can_message_in_job's classes is rewritten. PGlite (`PGLITE_DIR=~/.lh-pglite-probe npx tsx src/test/pglite/messageGateCurrentParty.pglite.mjs`, 89 checks, each migration 3x): every function is read from its effective definition through effectiveDefs/blankSqlComments. The crew removal is a real DELETE firing the real trg_sync_job_after_roster_departure (20260925140148, restated by 20260925154606), and the real trg_group_job_has_no_lead refuses a lead. The harness exits 1 on a tree without 20260925140148. BEFORE, the removed crew member, the rejected applicant and declined/expired offerees can message the poster and be messaged by them. AFTER, all of those are refused both ways, while the hired Helpr, pending and accepted applicants, a pending offeree and a current crew member are allowed both ways. The 24h post-completion window and the cancelled-job close are unchanged. The poster-first lock and the stranger refusals are unchanged. Grants were checked per role, and an empty database runs both migrations twice as a no-op. GUARDS: src/test/messageGateCurrentParty.test.ts (effective definitions; 7 @mutate lines on the newest migration) and src/lib/offJobGate.test.tsx (notice render, no role words, send-path and ChatView wiring on comment-blanked source; 4 @mutate lines). To verify on prod after db-deploy: pg_get_functiondef of public.can_message_in_job(uuid,uuid) contains `j.direct_offer_status = 'pending'` and `a.status IN ('pending', 'accepted')`; that of public.can_send_message_to_in_job(uuid,uuid) contains `AND NOT public.is_off_job(_job_id, _receiver)`; proacl: can_message_in_job and is_off_job {postgres, service_role}, can_send_message_to_in_job and get_off_job_thread_state {postgres, authenticated, service_role}. Needs an lh-authz-rls REVIEW-ONLY pass before landing. **Verified live 2026-09-27 (lane D, read-only prod SQL):** 20260925175953 + 20260925230845 in schema_migrations; can_message_in_job branch 4 requires a pending/accepted application and branch 2 direct_offer_status = 'pending'; the receiver gate has AND NOT public.is_off_job(_job_id, _receiver); is_off_job EXECUTE only postgres/service_role. Left: the lead's lh-authz-rls review record of 3bf782e83 (docs/reviews/sensitive-reviews.jsonl has only lh-money-escrow for it), so no marker. **2026-09-27 (lead): lh-authz-rls REVIEW-ONLY pass on 3bf782e83: VERDICT clean** (live defs match, SECDEF search_path=public, no anon/PUBLIC grant, INSERT policy checks both gates, no leak through get_off_job_thread_state, legit parties allowed, ownerless jobs fail closed; not measured: a live token-swap insert).
